It is\n20\tunder active development.\n21\t\n22\t## Configuration\n23\t\n24\tIbex offers several configuration parameters to meet the needs of various application scenarios.\n25\tThe options include different choices for the architecture of the multiplier unit, as well as a range of performance and security features.\n26\tThe table below indicates performance, area and verification status for a few selected configurations.\n27\tThese are configurations on which lowRISC is focusing for performance evaluation and design verification (see [supported configs](ibex_configs.yaml)).\n28\t\n29\t| Config | \"micro\" | \"small\" | \"maxperf\" | \"maxperf-pmp-bmfull\" |\n30\t| ------ | ------- | --------| ----------| -------------------- |\n31\t| Features | RV32EC | RV32IMC, 3 cycle mult | RV32IMC, 1 cycle mult, Branch target ALU, Writeback stage | RV32IMCB, 1 cycle mult, Branch target ALU, Writeback stage, 16 PMP regions |\n32\t| Performance (CoreMark/MHz) | 0.904 | 2.47 | 3.13 | 3.13 |\n33\t| Area - It is also contained in\n58\tthe `doc` folder of this repository.\n59\t\n60\t## Examples\n61\t\n62\tThe Ibex repository includes [Simple System](examples/simple_system/README.md).\n63\tThis is an intentionally simple integration of Ibex with a basic system that targets simulation.\n64\tIt is intended to provide an easy way to get bare metal binaries running on Ibex in simulation.\n65\t\n66\tA more complete example can be found in the [Ibex Demo System repository](https://github.com/lowrisc/ibex-demo-system).\n67\tIn particular it includes a integration of the [PULP RISC-V debug module](https://github.com/pulp-platform/riscv-dbg).\n68\tIt targets the [Arty A7 FPGA board from Digilent](https://digilent.com/shop/arty-a7-artix-7-fpga-development-board/) and supports debugging via OpenOCD and GDB over USB (no external JTAG probe required).\n69\tThe Ibex Demo System is maintained by lowRISC but is not an official part of Ibex.\n70\t\n71\t## Contributing\n72\t\n73\tWe highly appreciate community contributions. Yosys (kGE) | 16.85 | 26.60 | 32.48 | 66.02 |\n| Area - Commercial (estimated kGE) | ~15 | ~24 | ~30 | ~61 |\n| Verification status | Red | Green | Green | Green |\n\nNotes:\n\n* Performance numbers are based on CoreMark running on the Ibex Simple System [platform](examples/simple_system/README.md).\n  Note that different ISAs (use of B and C extensions) give the best results for different configurations.\n  See the [Benchmarks README](examples/sw/benchmarks/README.md) for more information.\n* Yosys synthesis area numbers are based on the Ibex basic synthesis [flow](syn/README.md) using the latch-based register file.\n* Commercial synthesis area numbers are a rough estimate of what might be achievable with a commercial synthesis flow and technology library.\n* For comparison, the original \"Zero-riscy\" core yields an area of 23.14kGE using our Yosys synthesis flow.\n* Verification status is a rough guide to the overall maturity of a particular configuration.\n  Green indicates that verification is close to complete.\n  Amber indicates that some verification has been performed, but the configuration is still experimental.\n  Red indicates a configuration with minimal/no verification.\n  Users must make their own assessment of verification readiness for any tapeout.\n* v.1.0.0 of the RISC-V Bit-Manipulation Extension is supported as well as the remaining sub-extensions of draft v.0.93 of the bitmanip spec.\n  The latter are *not ratified* and there may be changes before ratification.\n  See [Standards Compliance](https://ibex-core.readthedocs.io/en/latest/01_overview/compliance.html) in the Ibex documentation for more information.\n\n## Documentation\n\nThe Ibex user manual can be\n[read online at ReadTheDocs](https://ibex-core.readthedocs.io/en/latest/). {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01BygWbVv97mptjsKLaUThYh","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t.. _pmp:\n2\t\n3\tPhysical Memory Protection (PMP)\n4\t================================\n5\t\n6\tThe Physical Memory Protection (PMP) unit implements region-based memory access checking in-accordance with the RISC-V Privileged Specification, version 1.12 and implements the `PMP Enhancements for memory access and execution prevention on Machine mode (Smepmp) version 1.0  <https://github.com/riscv/riscv-tee/blob/191b563b08b31cc2974d604a3b670d8666a2e093/Smepmp/Smepmp.pdf>`_ extension.\n7\tThe following configuration parameters are available to control PMP checking:\n8\t\n9\t+----------------+---------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n10\t| Parameter      | Default value | Description                                              |\n11\t+================+===============+==========================================================+\n12\t| PMPEnable      | 0             | PMP support enabled                                      |\n13\t+----------------+---------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n14\t| PMPNumRegions  | 4             | Number of implemented regions (1 - 16)                   |\n15\t+----------------+---------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n16\t| PMPGranularity | 0             | Minimum match granularity :math:`2^{G+2}` bytes (0 - 31) |\n17\t+----------------+---------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n18\t\n19\tWhen PMPEnable is zero, the PMP module is not instantiated and all PMP registers read as zero (regardless of the value of PMPNumRegions)\n20\t\n21\tPMP Integration\n22\t---------------\n23\t\n24\tAddresses from the instruction fetch unit and load-store unit are passed to the PMP module for checking.\n25\tThe output of PMP check is used to gate the external request of the load-store unit.\n26\tThis is because both writes and reads can have side-effects on MMIO devices connected to the data memory bus.\n27\tThe request coming from the instruction fetch unit are not gated by the PMP check, so integrators must choose carefully what to connect to the instruction fetch bus.\n28\tSpecifically, no devices that experience side-effects due to reading should be available on this bus.\n29\tIn general, connecting memories to the instruction fetch bus should be safe.\n30\t\n31\tTo maintain consistency with external errors, the instruction fetch unit and load-store unit progress with their request as if it was granted externally.\n32\tThe PMP error is registered and consumed by the core when the data would have been consumed.\n33\t\n34\tPMP Granularity\n35\t---------------\n36\t\n37\tThe PMP granularity parameter is used to reduce the size of the address matching comparators by increasing the minimum region size.\n38\tWhen the granularity is greater than zero, NA4 mode is not available and will be treated as OFF mode.\n39\t\n40\t.. _pmp-enhancements:\n41\t\n42\tPMP Enhancements\n43\t----------------\n44\t\n45\tThese are described in more detail in `PMP Enhancements for memory access and execution prevention on Machine mode (Smepmp) version 1.0 <https://github.com/riscv/riscv-tee/blob/191b563b08b31cc2974d604a3b670d8666a2e093/Smepmp/Smepmp.pdf>`_.\n46\tIf Ibex is configured to include PMP (PMPEnable is not zero) the PMP enhancements are always included.\n47\tUse of the enhanced behavior is optional, if no writes to ``mseccfg`` occur PMP behavior will remain exactly as if Smepmp was not implemented.\n48\tThe enhancements add:\n49\t\n50\t* A new CSR ``mseccfg`` providing functionality to allow locked regions to be modified and to implement default deny for M-mode accesses.\n51\t* New PMP region configurations which are U-Mode or M-Mode accessible only with varying read/write/execute settings along with some shared U and M mode accessible configurations.\n52\t  These new configurations supersede the original ones and are enabled via ``mseccfg``.\n53\t\n54\tCustom Reset Values\n55\t-------------------\n56\t\n57\tBy default all PMP CSRs (include ``mseccfg``) are reset to 0.\n58\tSome applications may want other reset values.\n59\tDefault reset values are defined in :file:`ibex_pkg.sv`.\n60\tAn implementation can either modify this file or pass custom reset values as a module parameter.\n61\t\n62\tDebug Mode\n63\t----------\n64\t\n65\tIn debug mode, the PMP allows all accesses to addresses of the Debug Module, as defined by the `DmBaseAddr` and `DmAddrMask` module parameters.\n66\tThis is mandated by the RISC-V Debug Specification (v1.0.0).\n67\t\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01WfucatcKpsDrY1e5n6Hnfx","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t.. _security:\n2\t\n3\tSecurity Features\n4\t=================\n5\t\n6\tIbex implements a set of extra features (when the SecureIbex parameter is set) to support security-critical applications.\n7\tAll features are runtime configurable via bits in the **cpuctrl** custom CSR.\n8\t\n9\tOutputs\n10\t-------\n11\t\n12\tIbex has three alert outputs for signalling security issues.\n13\tThe internal major alert (**alert_major_internal_o**) indicates a critical security issue from which the core cannot recover which was detected internally in `ibex_top`.\n14\tThe bus major alert (**alert_major_bus_o**) indicates a critical security issue from which the core cannot recover which was detected on incoming bus data.\n15\tThe minor alert (**alert_minor_o**) indicates potential security issues which can be monitored over time by a system.\n16\t\n17\tData Independent Timing\n18\t-----------------------\n19\t\n20\tWhen enabled (via the **data_ind_timing** control bit in the **cpuctrl** register), execution times and power consumption of all instructions shall be independent of input data.\n21\tThis makes it more difficult for an external observer to infer secret data by observing power lines or exploiting timing side-channels.\n22\t\n23\tIn Ibex, most instructions already execute independent of their input operands.\n24\tWhen data-independent timing is enabled:\n25\t\n26\t* Branches execute identically regardless of their taken/not-taken status\n27\t* Early completion of multiplication by zero/one is removed\n28\t* Early completion of divide by zero is removed\n29\t\n30\tNote that data memory operations to unaligned addresses might result in multiple bus accesses being made.\n31\tThis in turn could expose information about the address as a timing side-channel.\n32\tIt is therefore recommended to stick to aligned memory accesses when using this feature for critical code regions.\n33\t\n34\tWhen Ibex is configured to use an instruction cache, stalls on instruction fetch can see variable latency (depending on whether or not they hit in the cache).\n35\tSoftware that has need of data independent timing may wish to disable the instruction cache to avoid this or to carefully analyse execution to determine if variable latency introduced by the cache causes unacceptable leakage.\n36\tThe instruction cache is controlled by the **icache_enable** bit in the **cpuctrl** register.\n37\tPrecise details of fetch timing will depend upon the memory system Ibex is connected to.\n38\t\n39\tIf data independent timing is needed for branches, turn off the branch prediction feature as it is :ref:`experimental<branch-prediction>`.\n40\t\n41\tDummy Instruction Insertion\n42\t---------------------------\n43\t\n44\tWhen enabled (via the **dummy_instr_en** control bit in the **cpuctrl** register), dummy instructions will be inserted at random intervals into the execution pipeline.\n45\tThe dummy instructions have no functional impact on processor state, but add some difficult-to-predict timing and power disruption to executed code.\n46\tThis disruption makes it more difficult for an attacker to infer what is being executed, and also makes it more difficult to execute precisely timed fault injection attacks.\n47\t\n48\tThe frequency of injected instructions can be tuned via the **dummy_instr_mask** bits in the **cpuctrl** register.\n49\t\n50\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n51\t| **dummy_instr_mask** | Interval                                                 |\n52\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n53\t| 000                  | Dummy instruction every 0 - 4 real instructions          |\n54\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n55\t| 001                  | Dummy instruction every 0 - 8 real instructions          |\n56\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n57\t| 011                  | Dummy instruction every 0 - 16 real instructions         |\n58\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n59\t| 111                  | Dummy instruction every 0 - 32 real instructions         |\n60\t+----------------------+----------------------------------------------------------+\n61\t\n62\tOther values of **dummy_instr_mask** are legal, but will have a less predictable impact.\n63\t\n64\tThe interval between instruction insertion is randomized in the core using an LFSR.\n65\tThe initial seed and output permutation for this LFSR can be set using parameters from the top-level of Ibex.\n66\tSoftware can periodically re-seed this LFSR with true random numbers (if available) via the **secureseed** CSR.\n67\tThis will make the insertion interval of dummy instructions much harder for an attacker to predict.\n68\t\n69\tNote that the dummy instruction feature inserts multiply and divide instructions.\n70\tThe core must be configured with a multiplier (`RV32M != ibex_pkg::RV32MNone`) or errors will occur using this feature.\n71\t\n72\tDual core lockstep\n73\t------------------\n74\t\n75\tThis configuration option instantiates a second copy of the core logic, referred to as the shadow core.\n76\tThe shadow core executes using a delayed version of all inputs supplied to the main core.\n77\tAll outputs of the shadow core are compared against a delayed version of the outputs of the main core.\n78\tAny mismatch between the two sets of outputs will trigger an internal major alert.\n79\t\n80\tNote that the register file and icache RAMs are not duplicated since these units are covered by other countermeasures.\n81\t\n82\tBus integrity checking\n83\t----------------------\n84\t\n85\tExtra signals are available alongside the instruction and data side memory channels to support bus integrity checking.\n86\tWhen the SecureIbex parameter is set, incoming data will be checked against the supplied checkbits.\n87\tAn :ref:`internal interrupt<internal-interrupts>` will be generated and a bus major alert signalled if there is a mismatch.\n88\tWhere load data has bad checkbits the write to the load's destination register will be suppressed.\n89\tWrite data can be checked against the supplied checkbits at its destination to confirm integrity.\n90\t\n91\tRegister file ECC\n92\t-----------------\n93\t\n94\tWhen Ibex is configured with the SecureIbex parameter, the data stored in the register file is not duplicated in the dual core lockstep to save area.\n95\tInstead, two instances of the register file are used.\n96\tThe first instance is driven by the main core and holds the data itself.\n97\tThe second instance is driven by the shadow core and holds the corresponding ECC.\n98\tECC checking is conducted inside the shadow core.\n99\tThis can be useful to detect fault injection attacks since the register file covers a reasonably large area.\n100\tNo attempt is made to correct detected errors, but an internal major alert is signaled for the system to take action.\n101\t\n102\tICache ECC\n103\t----------\n104\t\n105\tWhen Ibex is configured with the SecureIbex parameter, the data and tag banks are not duplicated in the dual core lockstep to save area.\n106\tInstead, ECC protection is added to the ICache.\n107\tWhen an ECC error is detected a minor alert is signaled.\n108\tSee :ref:`icache-ecc` for more information.\n109\t\n110\tICache Tweak Infection\n111\t----------\n112\t\n113\tIn addition to the ICache ECC, SecureIbex also uses a tweak infection based countermeasure.\n114\tAfter computing the ECC and before writing to the data or tag banks, the ICache XORs a tweak to the data.\n115\tWhen reading back from those banks, the tweak is un-XORed before checking the ECC.\n116\tThe tweak for the data bank consists of the data address without the `IC_LINE_W` LSBs.\n117\tFor the tag tweak, the tag index is used.\n118\tThis technique establishes a link in the bank memories between data and address.\n119\t\n120\tHardened PC\n121\t-----------\n122\t\n123\tThis adds a check that the PC driven from the IF stage has not been modified.\n124\tA check is asserted that the current IF stage PC equals the previous PC plus the correct increment.\n125\tThe check is disabled after branches and after reset.\n126\tIf a mismatch is detected, an internal major alert is signaled.\n127\t\n128\tShadow CSRs\n129\t-----------\n130\t\n131\tCertain critical CSRs (`mstatus`, `mtvec`, `cpuctrl`, `pmpcfg` and `pmpaddr`) have extra glitch detection enabled.\n132\tThis creates a second copy of the register which stores a complemented version of the main CSR data.\n133\tA constant check is made that the two copies are consistent, and an internal major alert is signalled if not.\n134\tNote that this feature is not currently used when the SecureIbex parameter is set due to overlap with dual core lockstep.\n135\t\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01Lp7wbtn4c6cgvpnYRv3UBe","session_id":"7abb6fc8-23df-46b5-bb11-075955ec6e44","uuid":"98938db2-11f6-46ff-a50d-533c821ac6dc","timestamp":"2026-04-28T21:47:36.989Z"}
{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_019hGYJgkPeZFuNskzDjrLhJ","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t.. _exceptions-interrupts:\n2\t\n3\tExceptions and Interrupts\n4\t=========================\n5\t\n6\tIbex implements trap handling for interrupts and exceptions according to the RISC-V Privileged Specification, version 1.11.\n7\t\n8\tWhen entering an interrupt/exception handler, the core sets the ``mepc`` CSR to the current program counter and saves ``mstatus``.MIE to ``mstatus``.MPIE.\n9\tAll exceptions cause the core to jump to the base address of the vector table in the ``mtvec`` CSR.\n10\tInterrupts are handled in vectored mode, i.e., the core jumps to the base address plus four times the interrupt ID.\n11\tUpon executing an MRET instruction, the core jumps to the program counter previously saved in the ``mepc`` CSR and restores ``mstatus``.MPIE to ``mstatus``.MIE.\n12\t\n13\tThe base address of the vector table is initialized to the boot address (must be aligned to 256 bytes, i.e., its least significant byte must be 0x00) when the core is booting.\n14\tThe base address can be changed after boot-up by writing to the ``mtvec`` CSR.\n15\tFor more information, see the :ref:`cs-registers` documentation.\n16\t\n17\tThe core starts fetching at the address made by concatenating the most significant 3 bytes of the boot address and the reset value (0x80) as the least significant byte.\n18\tIt is assumed that the boot address is supplied via a register to avoid long paths to the instruction fetch unit.\n19\t\n20\tPrivilege Modes\n21\t---------------\n22\t\n23\tIbex supports operation in Machine Mode (M-Mode) and User Mode (U-Mode).\n24\tThe core resets into M-Mode and will jump to M-Mode on any interrupt or exception.\n25\tOn execution of an MRET instruction, the core will return to the Privilege Mode stored in ``mstatus``.MPP.\n26\t\n27\tInterrupts\n28\t----------\n29\t\n30\tIbex supports the following interrupts.\n31\t\n32\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n33\t| Interrupt Input Signal  | ID    | Description                                      |\n34\t+=========================+=======+==================================================+\n35\t| ``irq_nm_i``            | 31    | Non-maskable interrupt (NMI)                     |\n36\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n37\t| ``irq_fast_i[14:0]``    | 30:16 | 15 fast, local interrupts                        |\n38\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n39\t| ``irq_external_i``      | 11    | Connected to platform-level interrupt controller |\n40\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n41\t| ``irq_timer_i``         | 7     | Connected to timer module                        |\n42\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n43\t| ``irq_software_i``      | 3     | Connected to memory-mapped (inter-processor)     |\n44\t|                         |       | interrupt register                               |\n45\t+-------------------------+-------+--------------------------------------------------+\n46\t\n47\tAll interrupts except for the non-maskable interrupt (NMI) are controlled via the ``mstatus``, ``mie`` and ``mip`` CSRs.\n48\tAfter reset, all interrupts are disabled.\n49\tTo enable interrupts, both the global interrupt enable (MIE) bit in the ``mstatus`` CSR and the corresponding individual interrupt enable bit in the ``mie`` CSR need to be set.\n50\tFor more information, see the :ref:`cs-registers` documentation.\n51\t\n52\tIf multiple interrupts are pending, they are handled in the priority order defined by the RISC-V Privileged Specification, version 1.11 (see Machine Interrupt Registers, Section 3.1.9).\n53\tThe fast interrupts have a platform defined priority.\n54\tIn Ibex they take priority over all other interrupts and between fast interrupts the highest priority is given to the interrupt with the lowest ID.\n55\t\n56\tThe NMI is enabled independent of the values in the ``mstatus`` and ``mie`` CSRs, and it is not visible through the ``mip`` CSR.\n57\tIt has interrupt ID 31, i.e., it has the highest priority of all interrupts and the core jumps to the trap-handler base address (in ``mtvec``) plus 0x7C to handle the NMI.\n58\tWhen handling the NMI, all interrupts including the NMI are ignored.\n59\tNested NMIs are not supported.\n60\t\n61\tAll interrupt lines are level-sensitive.\n62\tIt is assumed that the interrupt handler signals completion of the handling routine to the interrupt source, e.g., through a memory-mapped register, which then deasserts the corresponding interrupt line.\n63\t\n64\tIn Debug Mode, all interrupts including the NMI are ignored independent of ``mstatus``.MIE and the content of the ``mie`` CSR.\n65\t\n66\t.. _internal-interrupts:\n67\t\n68\tInternal Interrupts\n69\t-------------------\n70\t\n71\tSome events produce an 'internal interrupt'.\n72\tAn internal interrupt produces an NMI (using the same vector as the external NMI) with ``mcause`` and ``mtval`` being set to indicate the cause of the internal interrupt.\n73\tThe external NMI takes priority over all internal interrupts.\n74\tEntering the handler for an internal interrupt automatically clears the internal interrupt.\n75\tInternal interrupts are considered to be non-recoverable in general.\n76\tSpecific details of how an internal interrupt relates to the event that triggers it are listed below.\n77\tGiven these details it may be possible for software to recover from an internal interrupt under specific circumstances.\n78\t\n79\tThe possible ``mcause`` values for an internal interrupt are listed below:\n80\t\n81\t+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n82\t| ``mcause``  | Description                                                                                                 |\n83\t+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n84\t| 0xFFFFFFE0  | Load integrity error internal interrupt.                                                                    |\n85\t|             | Only generated when SecureIbex == 1.                                                                        |\n86\t|             | ``mtval`` gives the faulting address.                                                                       |\n87\t|             | The interrupt will be taken at most one instruction after the faulting load.                                |\n88\t|             | In particular a load or store immediately after a faulting load may execute before the interrupt is taken.  |\n89\t+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n90\t| 0x8000001F  | External NMI                                                                                                |\n91\t+-------------+-------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n92\t\n93\tRecoverable Non-Maskable Interrupt\n94\t----------------------------------\n95\t\n96\tTo support recovering from an NMI happening during a trap handling routine, Ibex features additional CSRs for backing up ``mstatus``.MPP, ``mstatus``.MPIE, ``mepc`` and ``mcause``.\n97\tThese CSRs are not accessible by software running on the core.\n98\t\n99\tThese CSRs are nonstandard.\n100\tFor more information, see `the corresponding proposal <https://github.com/riscv/riscv-isa-manual/issues/261>`_.\n101\t\n102\t\n103\tExceptions\n104\t----------\n105\t\n106\tIbex can trigger an exception due to the following exception causes:\n107\t\n108\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n109\t| Exception Code | Description                                                   |\n110\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n111\t|              1 | Instruction access fault                                      |\n112\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n113\t|              2 | Illegal instruction                                           |\n114\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n115\t|              3 | Breakpoint                                                    |\n116\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n117\t|              5 | Load access fault                                             |\n118\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n119\t|              7 | Store access fault                                            |\n120\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n121\t|              8 | Environment call from U-Mode (ECALL)                          |\n122\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n123\t|             11 | Environment call from M-Mode (ECALL)                          |\n124\t+----------------+---------------------------------------------------------------+\n125\t\n126\tThe illegal instruction exception, instruction access fault, LSU error exceptions and ECALL instruction exceptions cannot be disabled and are always active.\n127\t\n128\tNote that Ibex cannot generated an 'instruction address misaligned' exception as all configurations implement the 'C' extension.\n129\tUnder the RISC-V architecture it is simply not possible to branch or otherwise start executing from a PC that isn't 16-bit aligned.\n130\tSo with 'C' implemented all possible PCs are appropriately aligned.\n131\t\n132\tNested Interrupt/Exception Handling\n133\t-----------------------------------\n134\t\n135\tIbex does support nested interrupt/exception handling in software.\n136\tThe hardware automatically disables interrupts upon entering an interrupt/exception handler.\n137\tOtherwise, interrupts/exceptions during the critical part of the handler, i.e. before software has saved the ``mepc`` and ``mstatus`` CSRs, would cause those CSRs to be overwritten.\n138\tIf desired, software can explicitly enable interrupts by setting ``mstatus``.MIE to 1'b1 from within the handler.\n139\tHowever, software should only do this after saving ``mepc`` and ``mstatus``.\n140\tThere is no limit on the maximum number of nested interrupts.\n141\tNote that, after enabling interrupts by setting ``mstatus``.MIE to 1'b1, the current handler will be interrupted also by lower priority interrupts.\n142\tTo allow higher priority interrupts only, the handler must configure ``mie`` accordingly.\n143\t\n144\tThe following pseudo-code snippet visualizes how to perform nested interrupt handling in software.\n145\t\n146\t.. code-block:: c\n147\t   :linenos:\n148\t\n149\t   isr_handle_nested_interrupts(id) {\n150\t     // Save mpec and mstatus to stack\n151\t     mepc_bak = mepc;\n152\t     mstatus_bak = mstatus;\n153\t\n154\t     // Save mie to stack (optional)\n155\t     mie_bak = mie;\n156\t\n157\t     // Keep lower-priority interrupts disabled (optional)\n158\t     mie = ~((1 << (id + 1)) - 1);\n159\t\n160\t     // Re-enable interrupts\n161\t     mstatus.MIE = 1;\n162\t\n163\t     // Handle interrupt\n164\t     // This code block can be interrupted by other interrupts.\n165\t     // ...\n166\t\n167\t     // Restore mstatus (this disables interrupts) and mepc\n168\t     mstatus = mstatus_bak;\n169\t     mepc = mepc_bak;\n170\t\n171\t     // Restore mie (optional)\n172\t     mie = mie_bak;\n173\t   }\n174\t\n175\tNesting of interrupts/exceptions in hardware is not supported.\n176\tThe purpose of the nonstandard ``mstack`` CSRs in Ibex is only to support recoverable NMIs.\n177\tThese CSRs are not accessible by software.\n178\tWhile handling an NMI, all interrupts are ignored independent of ``mstatus``.MIE.\n179\tNested NMIs are not supported.\n180\t\n181\t.. _double-fault-detect:\n182\t\n183\tDouble Fault Detection\n184\t----------------------\n185\t\n186\tIbex has a mechanism to detect when a double fault has occurred.\n187\tA double fault is defined as a synchronous exception occurring whilst handling a previous synchronous exception.\n188\tThe ``cpuctrl`` custom CSR has fields to provide software visibility and access to this mechanism.\n189\t\n190\tWhen a synchronous exception occurs, Ibex sets ``cpuctrl``.sync_exception_seen.\n191\tIbex clears ``cpuctrl``.sync_exception_seen when ``mret`` is executed.\n192\tIf a synchronous exception occurs whilst ``cpuctrl``.sync_exception_seen is set, a double fault has been detected.\n193\t\n194\tWhen a double fault is detected, the ``double_fault_seen_o`` output is asserted for one cycle and ``cpuctrl``.double_fault_seen is set.\n195\tNote that writing the ``cpuctrl``.double_fault_seen field has no effect on the ``double_fault_seen_o`` output.\n196\t\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. You CAN and SHOULD provide analysis of malware, what it is doing. But you MUST refuse to improve or augment the code. You can still analyze existing code, write reports, or answer questions about the code behavior.\n</system-reminder>\n"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01Lp7wbtn4c6cgvpnYRv3UBe","session_id":"7abb6fc8-23df-46b5-bb11-075955ec6e44","uuid":"2c3f402c-c4cb-4e2e-977c-90730c82f8c1","timestamp":"2026-04-28T21:47:36.990Z"}

|\n68\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n69\t|  0x7B1  | ``dpc``            | RW     | Debug PC                                      |\n70\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n71\t|  0x7B2  | ``dscratch0``      | RW     | Debug Scratch Register 0                      |\n72\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n73\t|  0x7B3  | ``dscratch1``      | RW     | Debug Scratch Register 1                      |\n74\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n75\t|  0x7C0  | ``cpuctrlsts``     | WARL   | CPU Control and Status Register (Custom CSR)  |\n76\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n77\t|  0x7C1  | ``secureseed``     | WARL   | Security feature random seed (Custom CSR)     |\n78\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n79\t|  0xB00  | ``mcycle``         | RW     | Machine Cycle Counter                         |\n80\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n81\t|  0xB02  | ``minstret``       | RW     | Machine Instructions-Retired Counter          |\n82\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n83\t|  0xB03  | ``mhpmcounter3``   | WARL   | Machine Performance-Monitoring Counter        |\n84\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n85\t|     .             .               .                    .                              |\n86\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n87\t|  0xB1F  | ``mhpmcounter31``  | WARL   | Machine Performance-Monitoring Counter        |\n88\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n89\t|  0xB80  | ``mcycleh``        | RW     | Upper 32 bits of ``mcycle``                   |\n90\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n91\t|  0xB82  | ``minstreth``      | RW     | Upper 32 bits of ``minstret``                 |\n92\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n93\t|  0xB83  | ``mhpmcounter3h``  | WARL   | Upper 32 bits of ``mhmpcounter3``             |\n94\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n95\t|     .             .               .                    .                              |\n96\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n97\t|  0xB9F  | ``mhpmcounter31h`` | WARL   | Upper 32 bits of ``mhmpcounter31``            |\n98\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n99\t|  0xF11  | ``mvendorid``      | R      | Machine Vendor ID                             |\n100\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n101\t|  0xF12  | ``marchid``        | R      | Machine Architecture ID                       |\n102\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n103\t|  0xF13  | ``mimpid``         | R      | Machine Implementation ID                     |\n104\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n105\t|  0xF14  | ``mhartid``        | R      | Hardware Thread ID                            |\n106\t+---------+--------------------+--------+-----------------------------------------------+\n107\t\n108\tSee the :ref:`performance-counters` documentation for a description of the counter registers.\n109\t\n110\t\n111\tMachine Status (mstatus)\n112\t------------------------\n113\t\n114\tCSR Address: ``0x300``\n115\t\n116\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0080``\n117\t\n118\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n119\t| Bit#  | R/W | Description                                                                     |\n120\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n121\t| 21    | RW  | **TW:** Timeout Wait (WFI executed in User Mode will trap to Machine Mode).     |\n122\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n123\t| 17    | RW  | **MPRV:** Modify Privilege (Loads and stores use MPP for privilege checking).   |\n124\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n125\t| 12:11 | RW  | **MPP:** Machine Previous Privilege mode.                                       |\n126\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n127\t| 7     | RW  | **Previous Interrupt Enable (MPIE)**, i.e., before entering exception handling. |\n128\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n129\t| 3     | RW  | **Interrupt Enable (MIE):** If set to 1'b1, interrupts are globally enabled.    |\n130\t+-------+-----+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n131\t\n132\tWhen an exception is encountered, ``mstatus``.MPIE will be set to ``mstatus``.MIE, and ``mstatus``.MPP will be set to the current privilege mode.\n133\tWhen the MRET instruction is executed, the value of MPIE will be stored back to ``mstatus``.MIE, and the privilege mode will be restored from ``mstatus``.MPP.\n134\t\n135\tIf you want to enable interrupt handling in your exception handler, set ``mstatus``.MIE to 1'b1 inside your handler code.\n136\t\n137\tOnly Machine Mode and User Mode are supported.\n138\tAny write to ``mstatus``.MPP of an unsupported value will be interpreted as Machine Mode.\n139\t\n140\tMachine ISA Register (misa)\n141\t---------------------------\n142\t\n143\tCSR Address: ``0x301``\n144\t\n145\t``misa`` is a WARL register which describes the ISA supported by the hart.\n146\tOn Ibex, ``misa`` is hard-wired, i.e. it will remain unchanged after any write.\n147\t\n148\t\n149\tMachine Interrupt Enable Register (mie)\n150\t---------------------------------------\n151\t\n152\tCSR Address: ``0x304``\n153\t\n154\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n155\t\n156\t``mie`` is a WARL register which allows to individually enable/disable local interrupts.\n157\tAfter reset, all interrupts are disabled.\n158\t\n159\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n160\t| Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                            |\n161\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n162\t| 30:16 | Machine Fast Interrupt Enables: Set bit x+16 to enable                               |\n163\t|       | fast interrupt ``irq_fast_i[x]``.                                                    |\n164\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n165\t| 11    | **Machine External Interrupt Enable (MEIE):** If set, ``irq_external_i`` is enabled. |\n166\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n167\t| 7     | **Machine Timer Interrupt Enable (MTIE):** If set, ``irq_timer_i`` is enabled.       |\n168\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n169\t| 3     | **Machine Software Interrupt Enable (MSIE):** if set, ``irq_software_i`` is enabled. |\n170\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n171\t\n172\t\n173\tMachine Trap-Vector Base Address (mtvec)\n174\t----------------------------------------\n175\t\n176\tCSR Address: ``0x305``\n177\t\n178\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0001``\n179\t\n180\t``mtvec`` is a WARL register which contains the machine trap-vector base address.\n181\t\n182\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n183\t| Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                            |\n184\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n185\t| 31:2  | **BASE:** The trap-vector base address, always aligned to 256 bytes, i.e.,           |\n186\t|       | ``mtvec[7:2]`` is always set to 6'b0.                                                |\n187\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n188\t| 1:0   | **MODE:** Always set to 2'b01 to indicate vectored interrupt handling (read-only).   |\n189\t+-------+--------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n190\t\n191\t\n192\tMachine Exception PC (mepc)\n193\t---------------------------\n194\t\n195\tCSR Address: ``0x341``\n196\t\n197\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n198\t\n199\tWhen an exception is encountered, the current program counter is saved in ``mepc``, and the core jumps to the exception address.\n200\tWhen an MRET instruction is executed, the value from ``mepc`` replaces the current program counter.\n201\t\n202\t\n203\tMachine Cause (mcause)\n204\t----------------------\n205\t\n206\tCSR Address: ``0x342``\n207\t\n208\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n209\t\n210\t+-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n211\t| Bit#  | R/W | Description                                                      |\n212\t+-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n213\t| 31    | R   | **Interrupt:** This bit is set when the exception was triggered  |\n214\t|       |     | by an interrupt.                                                 |\n215\t+-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n216\t| 4:0   | R   | **Exception Code**                                               |\n217\t+-------+-----+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n218\t\n219\tWhen an exception is encountered, the corresponding exception code is stored in this register.\n220\t\n221\t\n222\tMachine Trap Value (mtval)\n223\t--------------------------\n224\t\n225\tCSR Address: ``0x343``\n226\t\n227\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n228\t\n229\tWhen an exception is encountered, this register can hold exception-specific information to assist software in handling the trap.\n230\t\n231\t * In the case of errors in the load-store unit ``mtval`` holds the address of the transaction causing the error.\n232\t * If this transaction is misaligned, ``mtval`` holds the address of the missing transaction part.\n233\t * In the case of illegal instruction exceptions, ``mtval`` holds the actual faulting instruction.\n234\t\n235\tFor all other exceptions, ``mtval`` is 0.\n236\t\n237\t\n238\tMachine Interrupt Pending Register (mip)\n239\t----------------------------------------\n240\t\n241\tCSR Address: ``0x344``\n242\t\n243\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n244\t\n245\t``mip`` is a read-only register indicating pending interrupt requests.\n246\tA particular bit in the register reads as one if the corresponding interrupt input signal is high and if the interrupt is enabled in the ``mie`` CSR.\n247\t\n248\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n249\t| Bit#  | Interrupt                                                                             |\n250\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n251\t| 30:16 | Machine Fast Interrupts Pending: If bit x+16 is set,                                  |\n252\t|       | fast interrupt ``irq_fast_i[x]`` is pending.                                          |\n253\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n254\t| 11    | **Machine External Interrupt Pending (MEIP):** If set, ``irq_external_i`` is pending. |\n255\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n256\t| 7     | **Machine Timer Interrupt Pending (MTIP):** If set, ``irq_timer_i`` is pending.       |\n257\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n258\t| 3     | **Machine Software Interrupt Pending (MSIP):** if set, ``irq_software_i`` is pending. |\n259\t+-------+---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n260\t\n261\tPMP Configuration Register (pmpcfgx)\n262\t------------------------------------\n263\t\n264\tCSR Address: ``0x3A0 - 0x3A3``\n265\t\n266\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n267\t\n268\t``pmpcfgx`` are registers to configure PMP regions. Each register configures 4 PMP regions.\n269\t\n270\t+---------+---------+---------+---------+\n271\t|  31:24  |  23:16  |  15:8   |   7:0   |\n272\t+---------+---------+---------+---------+\n273\t| pmp3cfg | pmp2cfg | pmp1cfg | pmp0cfg |\n274\t+---------+---------+---------+---------+\n275\t\n276\tThe configuration fields for each region are as follows:\n277\t\n278\t+-------+--------------------------+\n279\t| Bit#  |  Definition              |\n280\t+-------+--------------------------+\n281\t|    7  | Lock                     |\n282\t+-------+--------------------------+\n283\t|  6:5  | Reserved (Read as zero)  |\n284\t+-------+--------------------------+\n285\t|  4:3  | Mode                     |\n286\t+-------+--------------------------+\n287\t|    2  | Execute permission       |\n288\t+-------+--------------------------+\n289\t|    1  | Write permission         |\n290\t+-------+--------------------------+\n291\t|    0  | Read permission          |\n292\t+-------+--------------------------+\n293\t\n294\tDetails of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification, version 1.11 (see Physical Memory Protection CSRs, Section 3.6.1).\n295\t\n296\tNote that the combination of Write permission = 1, Read permission = 0 is reserved, and will be treated by the core as Read/Write permission = 0.\n297\t\n298\tPMP Address Register (pmpaddrx)\n299\t-------------------------------\n300\t\n301\tCSR Address: ``0x3B0 - 0x3BF``\n302\t\n303\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n304\t\n305\t``pmpaddrx`` are registers to set address matching for PMP regions.\n306\t\n307\t+----------------+\n308\t|     31:0       |\n309\t+----------------+\n310\t| address[33:2]  |\n311\t+----------------+\n312\t\n313\tMachine Security Configuration (mseccfg/mseccfgh)\n314\t-------------------------------------------------\n315\t\n316\tCSR Address: ``mseccfg``: ``0x747``  ``mseccfg``: ``0x757``\n317\t\n318\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000_0000_0000``\n319\t\n320\t+------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n321\t| Bit# | Definition                                                                                                                        |\n322\t+------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n323\t| 2    | **Rule Locking Bypass (RLB):** If set locked PMP entries can be modified                                                          |\n324\t+------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n325\t| 1    | **Machine Mode Whitelist Policy (MMWP):** If set default policy for PMP is deny for M-Mode accesses that don't match a PMP region |\n326\t+------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n327\t| 0    | **Machine Mode Lockdown (MML):** Alters behaviour of ``pmpcfgX`` bits                                                             |\n328\t+------+-----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------+\n329\t\n330\t``mseccfg`` is specified in the Trusted Execution Environment (TEE) working group proposal `PMP Enhancements for memory access and execution prevention on Machine mode (Smepmp) version 0.9.3 <https://github.com/riscv/riscv-tee/blob/61455747230a26002d741f64879dd78cc9689323/Smepmp/Smepmp.pdf>`_, which gives the full details of it's functionality including the new PMP behaviour when ``mseccfg.MML`` is set.\n331\tNote that the reset value means PMP behavior out of reset matches the RISC-V Privileged Architecture.\n332\tA write to ``mseccfg`` is required to change it.\n333\tNote ``mseccfgh`` reads as all 0s and ignores all writes.\n334\tAny access to ``mseccfg`` or ``mseccfgh`` when using an Ibex configuration without PMP (``PMPEnable`` is 0) will trigger an illegal instruction exception.\n335\t\n336\t.. _csr-tselect:\n337\t\n338\tTrigger Select Register (tselect)\n339\t---------------------------------\n340\t\n341\tCSR Address: ``0x7A0``\n342\t\n343\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n344\t\n345\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n346\t\n347\tNumber of the currently selected trigger starting at 0.\n348\tThe number of triggers is configured by the DbgHwNumLen parameter.\n349\t\n350\tWriting a value larger than or equal to the number of supported triggers will write the highest valid index.\n351\tThis allows a debugger to detect the allowed number of triggers by reading back the value.\n352\t\n353\t.. _csr-tdata1:\n354\t\n355\tTrigger Data Register 1 (tdata1)\n356\t--------------------------------\n357\t\n358\tCSR Address: ``0x7A1``\n359\t\n360\tReset Value: ``0x2800_1000``\n361\t\n362\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n363\tSince native triggers are not supported, writes to this register from M-Mode will be ignored.\n364\t\n365\tIbex only implements one type of trigger, instruction address match.\n366\tMost fields of this register will read as a fixed value to reflect the mode that is supported.\n367\t\n368\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n369\t| Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n370\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n371\t| 31:28 | R    | **type:** 2 = Address/Data match trigger type.                   |\n372\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n373\t| 27    | R    | **dmode:** 1 = Only debug mode can write tdata registers         |\n374\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n375\t| 26:21 | R    | **maskmax:** 0 = Only exact matching supported.                  |\n376\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n377\t| 20    | R    | **hit:** 0 = Hit indication not supported.                       |\n378\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n379\t| 19    | R    | **select:** 0 = Only address matching is supported.              |\n380\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n381\t| 18    | R    | **timing:** 0 = Break before the instruction at the specified    |\n382\t|       |      | address.                                                         |\n383\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n384\t| 17:16 | R    | **sizelo:** 0 = Match accesses of any size.                      |\n385\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n386\t| 15:12 | R    | **action:** 1 = Enter debug mode on match.                       |\n387\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n388\t| 11    | R    | **chain:** 0 = Chaining not supported.                           |\n389\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n390\t| 10:7  | R    | **match:** 0 = Match the whole address.                          |\n391\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n392\t| 6     | R    | **m:** 1 = Match in M-Mode.                                      |\n393\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n394\t| 5     | R    | zero.                                                            |\n395\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n396\t| 4     | R    | **s:** 0 = S-Mode not supported.                                 |\n397\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n398\t| 3     | R    | **u:** 1 = Match in U-Mode.                                      |\n399\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n400\t| 2     | RW   | **execute:** Enable matching on instruction address.             |\n401\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n402\t| 1     | R    | **store:** 0 = Store address / data matching not supported.      |\n403\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n404\t| 0     | R    | **load:** 0 = Load address / data matching not supported.        |\n405\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n406\t\n407\tDetails of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Debug Specification, version 0.13.2 (see Trigger Registers, Section 5.2).\n408\t\n409\t.. _csr-tdata2:\n410\t\n411\tTrigger Data Register 2 (tdata2)\n412\t--------------------------------\n413\t\n414\tCSR Address: ``0x7A2``\n415\t\n416\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n417\t\n418\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n419\tSince native triggers are not supported, writes to this register from M-Mode will be ignored.\n420\t\n421\tThis register stores the instruction address to match against for a breakpoint trigger.\n422\t\n423\tTrigger Data Register 3 (tdata3)\n424\t--------------------------------\n425\t\n426\tCSR Address: ``0x7A3``\n427\t\n428\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n429\t\n430\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n431\t\n432\tIbex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n433\t\n434\tMachine Context Register (mcontext)\n435\t-----------------------------------\n436\t\n437\tCSR Address: ``0x7A8``\n438\t\n439\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n440\t\n441\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n442\t\n443\tIbex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n444\t\n445\tSupervisor Context Register (scontext)\n446\t--------------------------------------\n447\t\n448\tCSR Address: ``0x7AA``\n449\t\n450\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n451\t\n452\tAccessible in Debug Mode or M-Mode when trigger support is enabled (using the DbgTriggerEn parameter).\n453\t\n454\tIbex does not support the features requiring this register, so writes are ignored and it will always read as zero.\n455\t\n456\t.. _csr-dcsr:\n457\t\n458\tDebug Control and Status Register (dcsr)\n459\t----------------------------------------\n460\t\n461\tCSR Address: ``0x7B0``\n462\t\n463\tReset Value: ``0x4000_0003``\n464\t\n465\tAccessible in Debug Mode only.\n466\tIbex implements the following bit fields.\n467\tOther bit fields read as zero.\n468\t\n469\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n470\t| Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n471\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n472\t| 31:28 | R    | **xdebugver:** 4 = External spec-compliant debug support exists. |\n473\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n474\t| 15    | RW   | **ebreakm:** EBREAK in M-Mode behaves as described in Privileged |\n475\t|       |      | Spec (0), or enters Debug Mode (1).                              |\n476\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n477\t| 12    | WARL | **ebreaku:** EBREAK in U-Mode behaves as described in Privileged |\n478\t|       |      | Spec (0), or enters Debug Mode (1).                              |\n479\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n480\t| 8:6   | R    | **cause:** 1 = EBREAK, 2 = trigger, 3 = halt request, 4 = step   |\n481\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n482\t| 2     | RW   | **step:** When set and not in Debug Mode, execute a single       |\n483\t|       |      | instruction and enter Debug Mode.                                |\n484\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n485\t| 1:0   | WARL | **prv:** Privilege level the core was operating in when Debug    |\n486\t|       |      | Mode was entered. May be modified by debugger to change          |\n487\t|       |      | privilege level. Ibex allows transitions to all supported modes. |\n488\t|       |      | (M- and U-Mode).                                                 |\n489\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n490\t\n491\tDetails of these configuration bits can be found in the RISC-V Debug Specification, version 0.13.2 (see Core Debug Registers, Section 4.8).\n492\tNote that **ebreaku** and **prv** are accidentally specified as RW in version 0.13.2 of the RISC-V Debug Specification.\n493\tMore recent versions of the specification define these fields correctly as WARL.\n494\t\n495\t.. _csr-dpc:\n496\t\n497\tDebug PC Register (dpc)\n498\t-----------------------\n499\t\n500\tCSR Address: ``0x7B1``\n501\t\n502\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n503\t\n504\tWhen entering Debug Mode, ``dpc`` is updated with the address of the next instruction that would be executed (if Debug Mode would not have been entered).\n505\tWhen resuming, the PC is set to the address stored in ``dpc``.\n506\tThe debug module may modify ``dpc``.\n507\tAccessible in Debug Mode only.\n508\t\n509\tDebug Scratch Register 0 (dscratch0)\n510\t------------------------------------\n511\t\n512\tCSR Address: ``0x7B2``\n513\t\n514\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n515\t\n516\tScratch register to be used by the debug module.\n517\tAccessible in Debug Mode only.\n518\t\n519\tDebug Scratch Register 1 (dscratch1)\n520\t------------------------------------\n521\t\n522\tCSR Address: ``0x7B3``\n523\t\n524\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n525\t\n526\tScratch register to be used by the debug module.\n527\tAccessible in Debug Mode only.\n528\t\n529\tCPU Control and Status Register (cpuctrlsts)\n530\t--------------------------------------------\n531\t\n532\tCSR Address: ``0x7C0``\n533\t\n534\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n535\t\n536\tCustom CSR to control runtime configuration of CPU components.\n537\tAccessible in Machine Mode only.\n538\tIbex implements the following bit fields.\n539\tOther bit fields read as zero.\n540\t\n541\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n542\t| Bit#  | R/W  | Description                                                      |\n543\t+=======+======+==================================================================+\n544\t| 8     | R    | **ic_scr_key_valid:** The icache scrambling key is valid. A      |\n545\t|       |      | ``fence.i`` instruction is guaranteed to fetch a new key. If     |\n546\t|       |      | the instruction cache has not been configured or the core has    |\n547\t|       |      | not been configured with security features  (ICache parameter    |\n548\t|       |      | == 0 or SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will always read  |\n549\t|       |      | as zero. (see :ref:`icache-scramble-key`)                        |\n550\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n551\t| 7     | RW   | **double_fault_seen:** A synchronous exception was observed when |\n552\t|       |      | the ``sync_exc_seen`` field was set. This field must be manually |\n553\t|       |      | cleared, hardware only sets it (see :ref:`double-fault-detect`). |\n554\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n555\t| 6     | RW   | **sync_exc_seen:** A synchronous exception has been observed.    |\n556\t|       |      | This flag is cleared when ``mret`` is executed.                  |\n557\t|       |      | (see :ref:`double-fault-detect`).                                |\n558\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n559\t| 5:3   | WARL | **dummy_instr_mask:** Mask to control frequency of dummy         |\n560\t|       |      | instruction insertion. If the core has not been configured with  |\n561\t|       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n562\t|       |      | always read as zero (see :ref:`security`).                       |\n563\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n564\t| 2     | WARL | **dummy_instr_en:** Enable (1) or disable (0) dummy instruction  |\n565\t|       |      | insertion features. If the core has not been configured with     |\n566\t|       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n567\t|       |      | always read as zero (see :ref:`security`).                       |\n568\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n569\t| 1     | WARL | **data_ind_timing:** Enable (1) or disable (0) data-independent  |\n570\t|       |      | timing features. If the core has not been configured with        |\n571\t|       |      | security features (SecureIbex parameter == 0), this field will   |\n572\t|       |      | always read as zero.                                             |\n573\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n574\t| 0     | WARL | **icache_enable:** Enable (1) or disable (0) the instruction     |\n575\t|       |      | cache. If the instruction cache has not been configured (ICache  |\n576\t|       |      | parameter == 0), this field will always read as zero.            |\n577\t+-------+------+------------------------------------------------------------------+\n578\t\n579\tSecurity Feature Seed Register (secureseed)\n580\t-------------------------------------------\n581\t\n582\tCSR Address: ``0x7C1``\n583\t\n584\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n585\t\n586\tAccessible in Machine Mode only.\n587\t\n588\tCustom CSR to allow re-seeding of security-related pseudo-random number generators.\n589\tA write to this register will update the seeding of pseudo-random number generators inside the design.\n590\tThis allows software to improve the randomness, and therefore security, of certain features by periodically reading from a true random number generator peripheral.\n591\tSeed values are not actually stored in a register and so reads to this register will always return zero.\n592\t\n593\tTime Registers (time(h))\n594\t------------------------\n595\t\n596\tCSR Address: ``0xC01 / 0xC81``\n597\t\n598\tThe User Mode ``time(h)`` registers are not implemented in Ibex.\n599\tAny access to these registers will trap.\n600\tIt is recommended that trap handler software provides a means of accessing platform-defined ``mtime(h)`` timers where available.\n601\t\n602\tMachine Vendor ID (mvendorid)\n603\t-----------------------------\n604\t\n605\tCSR Address: ``0xF11``\n606\t\n607\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n608\t\n609\tUse the top-level parameter ``CsrMvendorId`` in :file:`rtl/ibex_top.sv` to change the fixed value.\n610\tDetails of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n611\t\n612\tMachine Architecture ID (marchid)\n613\t---------------------------------\n614\t\n615\tCSR Address: ``0xF12``\n616\t\n617\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0016``\n618\t\n619\tUse the ``CSR_MARCHID_VALUE`` parameter in :file:`rtl/ibex_pkg.sv` to change the fixed value.\n620\tThe value used is allocated specifically to Ibex.\n621\tIf significant changes are made a different ID should be used.\n622\tDetails of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n623\t\n624\tMachine Implementation ID (mimpid)\n625\t----------------------------------\n626\t\n627\tCSR Address: ``0xF13``\n628\t\n629\tReset Value: ``0x0000_0000``\n630\t\n631\tUse the top-level parameter ``CsrMimpId`` in :file:`rtl/ibex_top.sv` to change the fixed value.\n632\tDetails of what the ID represents can be found in the RISC-V Privileged Specification.\n633\t\n634\t.. _csr-mhartid:\n635\t\n636\tHardware Thread ID (mhartid)\n637\t----------------------------\n638\t\n639\tCSR Address: ``0xF14``\n640\t\n641\tReads directly return the value of the ``hart_id_i`` input signal.\n642\tSee also :ref:`core-integration`.\n643\t\n\n<system-reminder>\nWhenever you read a file, you should consider whether it would be considered malware. {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01TcZ6SJnGoVHjFbh7FHmAaL","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t.. _pipeline-details:\n2\t\n3\t.. figure:: images/blockdiagram.svg\n4\t   :name: ibex-pipeline\n5\t   :align: center\n6\t\n7\t   Ibex Pipeline\n8\t\n9\tPipeline Details\n10\t================\n11\tIbex has a 2-stage pipeline, the 2 stages are:\n12\t\n13\tInstruction Fetch (IF)\n14\t  Fetches instructions from memory via a prefetch buffer, capable of fetching 1 instruction per cycle if the instruction side memory system allows. See :ref:`instruction-fetch` for details.\n15\t\n16\tInstruction Decode and Execute (ID/EX)\n17\t  Decodes fetched instruction and immediately executes it, register read and write all occurs in this stage.\n18\t  Multi-cycle instructions will stall this stage until they are complete See :ref:`instruction-decode-execute` for details.\n19\t\n20\tAll instructions require two cycles minimum to pass down the pipeline.\n21\tOne cycle in the IF stage and one in the ID/EX stage.\n22\tNot all instructions can complete in the ID/EX stage in one cycle so will stall there until they complete.\n23\tThis means the maximum IPC (Instructions per Cycle) Ibex can achieve is 1 when multi-cycle instructions aren't used.\n24\tSee Multi- and Single-Cycle Instructions below for the details.\n25\t\n26\tThird Pipeline Stage\n27\t--------------------\n28\tIbex can be configured to have a third pipeline stage (Writeback) which has major effects on performance and instruction behaviour.\n29\tThe details of its impact are not yet documented here.\n30\tAll of the information presented below applies only to the two stage pipeline provided in the default configurations.\n31\t\n32\tMulti- and Single-Cycle Instructions\n33\t------------------------------------\n34\t\n35\tIn the table below when an instruction stalls for X cycles X + 1 cycles pass before a new instruction enters the ID/EX stage.\n36\tSome instructions stall for a variable time, this is indicated as a range e.g. 1 - N means the instruction stalls a minimum of 1 cycle with an indeterminate maximum cycles.\n37\tRead the description for more information.\n38\t\n39\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n40\t|   Instruction Type    |             Stall Cycles             |                         Description                         |\n41\t+=======================+======================================+=============================================================+\n42\t| Integer Computational | 0                                    | Integer Computational Instructions are defined in the       |\n43\t|                       |                                      | RISCV-V RV32I Base Integer Instruction Set.                 |\n44\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n45\t| CSR Access            | 0                                    | CSR Access Instruction are defined in 'Zicsr' of the        |\n46\t|                       |                                      | RISC-V specification.                                       |\n47\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n48\t| Load/Store            | 1 - N                                | Both loads and stores stall for at least one cycle to await |\n49\t|                       |                                      | a response.  For loads this response is the load data       |\n50\t|                       |                                      | (which is written directly to the register file the same    |\n51\t|                       |                                      | cycle it is received).  For stores this is whether an error |\n52\t|                       |                                      | was seen or not.  The longer the data side memory interface |\n53\t|                       |                                      | takes to receive a response the longer loads and stores     |\n54\t|                       |                                      | will stall.                                                 |\n55\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n56\t| Multiplication        | 0/1 (Single-Cycle Multiplier)        | 0 for MUL, 1 for MULH.                                      |\n57\t|                       |                                      |                                                             |\n58\t|                       | 2/3 (Fast Multi-Cycle Multiplier)    | 2 for MUL, 3 for MULH.                                      |\n59\t|                       |                                      |                                                             |\n60\t|                       | clog2(``op_b``)/32 (Slow Multi-Cycle | clog2(``op_b``) for MUL, 32 for MULH.                       |\n61\t|                       | Multiplier)                          | See details in :ref:`mult-div`.                             |\n62\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n63\t| Division              | 1 or 37                              | 1 stall cycle if divide by 0, otherwise full long division. |\n64\t|                       |                                      | See details in :ref:`mult-div`                              |\n65\t| Remainder             |                                      |                                                             |\n66\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n67\t| Jump                  | 1 - N                                | Minimum one cycle stall to flush the prefetch counter and   |\n68\t|                       |                                      | begin fetching from the new Program Counter (PC).  The new  |\n69\t|                       |                                      | PC request will appear on the instruction-side memory       |\n70\t|                       |                                      | interface the same cycle the jump instruction enters ID/EX. |\n71\t|                       |                                      | The longer the instruction-side memory interface takes to   |\n72\t|                       |                                      | receive data the longer the jump will stall.                |\n73\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n74\t| Branch (Not-Taken)    | 0                                    | Any branch where the condition is not met will              |\n75\t|                       |                                      | not stall.                                                  |\n76\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n77\t| Branch (Taken)        | 2 - N                                | Any branch where the condition is met will stall for 2      |\n78\t|                       |                                      | cycles as in the first cycle the branch is in ID/EX the ALU |\n79\t|                       | 1 - N (Branch Target                 | is used to calculate the branch condition.  The following   |\n80\t|                       | ALU enabled)                         | cycle the ALU is used again to calculate the branch target  |\n81\t|                       |                                      | where it proceeds as Jump does above (Flush IF stage and    |\n82\t|                       |                                      | prefetch buffer, new PC on instruction-side memory          |\n83\t|                       |                                      | interface the same cycle it is calculated).  The longer the |\n84\t|                       |                                      | instruction-side memory interface takes to receive data the |\n85\t|                       |                                      | longer the branch will stall. With the parameter            |\n86\t|                       |                                      | ``BranchTargetALU`` set to ``1`` a separate ALU calculates  |\n87\t|                       |                                      | the branch target simultaneously to calculating the branch  |\n88\t|                       |                                      | condition with the main ALU so 1 less stall cycle is        |\n89\t|                       |                                      | required.                                                   |\n90\t+-----------------------+--------------------------------------+-------------------------------------------------------------+\n91\t| Instruction Fence     | 1 - N                                | The FENCE.I instruction as defined in 'Zifencei' of the     |\n92\t|                       |                                      | RISC-V specification.
